## What this is

Ports refreshed agent instructions and run-record hygiene from `dev_sprint` onto `dev`: branch-tier workflow (direct commits on `dev_sprint`, draft PRs on `dev`), reduced context burn for tier S/M work, and archived inactive run YAML under `agent_rules/runs/closed/`.

@kungfusaini

## Technical detail

### Summary

- Reintroduce `AGENTS.md`, `agent_rules/` workflow docs, and `.cursorignore` on `dev`.
- Add `tools/dev/archive_inactive_runs.py` and move inactive run records to `runs/closed/`.
- Trim `runs/INDEX.md` to `in_progress` / `blocked` only.

### Approach

Cherry-picked `4a7249c` and `b286f2d` from `dev_sprint` onto `agent-workflow-context-refresh` off `dev`. Resolved modify/delete conflicts (agent_rules absent on `dev`) by keeping incoming files; resolved archive rename/delete conflicts via `git checkout b286f2d -- agent_rules/runs/`.

### Tests

- `uv run python -m tools.dev.agent_rules_lint` (script from `dev_sprint`; not in this PR diff) — passed locally.

### Verification

- Agent-rules lint passed before push.
- Awaiting required GitHub checks on draft PR.

### Risks

- Large run-record import re-establishes process metadata on `dev`; active INDEX lists only blocked/in_progress runs.
- `dev` previously lacked `agent_rules/`; consumers should follow updated branch workflow.

### Branch-merge

- Source: `dev_sprint` commits `4a7249c`, `b286f2d`.
- Target: `dev` via draft PR (no direct commits to `dev`).

### Out of scope (deliberately)

- Product/runtime code from `dev_sprint`.
- `tools/dev/agent_rules_lint.py` (exists on `dev_sprint`; not part of port commits).

- Cherry-picks `4a7249c` + `b286f2d` from `dev_sprint` to add `AGENTS.md`, `agent_rules/` workflow docs, `.cursorignore`.

selected_scope:
  summary: >
    Land five methodology fixes on the FS4 crude overfit probe so the next
    live run produces trustworthy signal: (B1) refresh run.toml heartbeat
    per row instead of only at probe start/end; (B3) persist every raw
    Anthropic structured response per generation attempt for forensic use;
    (F2) trace and either fix or document the defensibility silent-fallback
    claim; (B2) soft-fail a single iteration when retry exhaustion strikes
    candidate generation, keep current incumbent, abort only if more than
    three iterations skip; (F3) expose a probe-progress endpoint and have
    the Operator Lab UI poll it while the probe is running. All changes are
    unconditional behaviour; no optional toggles or callbacks.
  included_plan_sections:
    - B1 per-row heartbeat in run.toml.
    - B3 raw_response_attempt_NN.json under iteration directories.
    - F2 verification (and fix only if a silent fallback is real).
    - B2 batch_skipped accounting with 3-of-10 abort threshold.
    - F3 GET /fs10/operator-lab/overfit-probes/{run_id}/progress and frontend polling.
  out_of_scope:
    - Promotion or production prompt application.
    - Generalizing the probe to non-FS4 surfaces.
    - F1, F4, F5 from the methodology report (will land in a follow-up slice if needed).
  acceptance_criteria:
    - last_heartbeat_at in run.toml advances at least every 30 seconds during a probe.
    - Every Anthropic candidate-generation attempt persists a raw_response_attempt_NN.json.
    - Defensibility scoring either no longer silently substitutes a worst-case score, or it explicitly flags fallback rows and the final audit counts them.
    - A single iteration that exhausts retries records a batch_skipped diagnostic and the probe continues; three skips abort with fs10_fs4_crude_fit_probe_too_many_skipped_batches.
    - Frontend can poll a progress endpoint and surface live phase/row counters during a probe.
    - Targeted FS4 / overfit / fit_probe pytest selection passes and ruff is clean.

        defensibility is unavailable (line 2607). evaluate_operator_lab_candidates
        catches the exception, marks the row status='failed', calls _failed_row_score
        (worst-case composite 1.0 for minimize metrics) as the row's score record,
        writes a visible fs4_crude_scoring_failure.json, and propagates
        failure_class. _fit_probe_real_metrics_ok flags any row with status='failed'
        which fails the probe verdict's all_metrics_pass. Worst-case score ends up
        in aggregate mean but the probe verdict is already failed, so the polluted
        mean does not feed into a promotion decision. F2 is a no-op; no code change.

# Agent Instructions

These instructions apply to coding agents (Cursor, Claude Code, Codex) in this
repository. Follow them unless the user gives a newer explicit instruction in
the conversation.

## Canonical instruction source

- `AGENTS.md` is the canonical shared instruction file.
- Codex, Claude Code, and Cursor read `AGENTS.md` directly.
- Do not duplicate these rules into `.cursor/rules/`, `CLAUDE.md`, or other
  agent instruction files.
- Keep this file concise. Long protocols, skills, schemas, and run records live
  under `agent_rules/`.

## Branch workflow (detect first)

Run `git branch --show-current` before any git or PR action.

| Branch | Default workflow |
| --- | --- |
| `dev_sprint` | Edit → minimal verify → **commit directly** to `dev_sprint`. No PR unless the user asks. |
| `dev` | Edit on a **feature branch** off `dev` → minimal verify → push → open a **draft** PR targeting `dev`. **Do not commit directly to `dev`** unless the user explicitly asks. |
| other | Stop and confirm the intended branch with the user. |

Porting selected capabilities from `dev_sprint` into `dev` is a separate flow
(capability diff / port skills), not ordinary work on `dev`.

### PRs targeting `dev`

- **Always create PRs as draft** (`gh pr create --draft` or equivalent).
- **All required GitHub checks must pass** before the PR is eligible to leave
  draft. Fix failures, push, and re-check until green.
- Mark ready for review only after required GitHub checks are green (see
  `agent_rules/pr_workflow.md`).
- Never merge a PR with failing or pending required checks.
- Always tag `@kungfusaini` on every PR.

`dev_sprint` rarely uses PRs. Do not open PRs to `dev_sprint` unless the user
asks.

## Task tiers (context discipline)

Choose a tier from the user prompt. Default to **S** when unclear.

| Tier | When | Required |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **S** | Single area, roughly ≤5 files, user named the change | Branch rule, `git status --short`, read named paths, **first code edit within the first few tool rounds**, minimal verify, commit or draft PR per branch |
| **M** | Multi-file feature, one session | Tier S + short plan (≤5 bullets) in chat; targeted tests; no new run record unless multi-session |
| **L** | Cross-FS, gated, multi-session, Codex batches | Full `agent_rules/run_protocol.md`, run record, skills as needed, full `pr_workflow.md` before PR push |

### Delivery rules (all tiers)

- **Done** means working code in the repo (commit on `dev_sprint` or draft PR on
  `dev`), with verification run for the touched area — not plans, run-record
  essays, or checklists recited in chat.
- Do **not** create or update `agent_rules/runs/` for tier **S** unless the user
  names a run id.
- Do **not** read `docs/plans/` before the first edit unless the user asked for
  design review or tier **L** planning.
- Prefer ≤3 file reads before the first edit unless locating a symbol.
- Keep status messages short between tool batches; put long analysis in files
  only when the user asked for a report.

### Code bloat (mandatory)

- **Minimal diff**: solve the asked problem with the smallest correct change.
- **No drive-by refactors**, renames, formatting sweeps, or unrelated cleanup.
- **No new abstractions** (helpers, base classes, indirection) unless the user
  asked or the change cannot be done cleanly inline.
- **Reuse existing** functions, patterns, and types; extend before adding parallel
  implementations.
- **Do not expand scope** to “while we’re here” items without explicit user
  approval.
- **One logical change** per commit or PR.

## Pointers (tier-gated)

| Topic | Read when |
| --- | --- |

> AGENT

Here's what's actually in PR #271:

**100 files, +39,489 lines.** Breakdown:

**1. Agent-instruction files (5)**
- `AGENTS.md` — a canonical agent rulebook (`Cursor`, `Claude Code`, `Codex` all read it). Introduces a branch-tier workflow: `dev_sprint` = commit directly / no PR; `dev` = feature-branch + draft PR. Adds task tiers S/M/L gating how much ceremony to perform.
- `.cursorignore`
- `agent_rules/README.md`, `cursor_session.md`, `pr_workflow.md`, `run_protocol.md`

**2. Active "run records" (7 YAML)** under `agent_rules/runs/`, plus `INDEX.md`. Only `blocked` and `in_progress` runs.

**3. Closed/archived run records (~85 YAML)** under `agent_rules/runs/closed/` — this is where the bulk of those 39k lines comes from.

**What the YAML actually is:** a structured "run record" schema (`schema_version: 1`) for tracking multi-step agent work. Each file has:
- `run` metadata (id, title, status, branch, timestamps, supersedes chain)
- `source_artifacts` (paths the agent read, with roles like `authoritative` / `failure evidence`)
- `selected_scope` (summary, included/out-of-scope, acceptance criteria)
- `coordination` (owned paths/subsystems, parallel-agent notes)
- `parallel_execution` (lanes / orchestrator)
- `verification` (required gates with exact commands)
- `execution_state` (assumptions, open/resolved questions, deltas, blockers)
- `quality` (confidence scores, residual risk)
- `handoff` (current state, next steps, summary for next agent)

Basically it's a **journal format for agent sessions** — every meaningful run on `dev_sprint` gets one of these YAMLs so a future agent can resume or audit it. The PR is porting that whole methodology + the historical archive from `dev_sprint` onto `dev`.

**My read:** it's not malicious or junk — it's a real process artifact from the `dev_sprint` workflow. But it's a 39k-line bulk import of mostly *historical* process metadata that has no runtime effect on the product. The "tests passed" claim is unverifiable (the lint script isn't in the diff). Fine to leave for Patrick/@kungfusaini to decide whether `dev` actually wants this archive.

> DEVELOPER

okay but whatabout eh database why are we using yaml?

> AGENT

Honest answer: it's a category violation against your own stated architecture, and yes it should bother you.

**What these YAMLs really are** — look at the schema: `schema_version: 1`, a `status` enum (`in_progress`/`blocked`/`verified`/`superseded`/`deferred`/`implemented`), `supersedes` / `superseded_by` (self-referential FK), `orchestrator_run_id` + `lane_id` (FKs to a parent run), `updated_at` timestamps, and a hand-maintained `INDEX.md` that filters by status.

That's a `runs` table. With foreign keys. And a hand-edited materialized view. Written as 85 YAML files.

**Why the author probably did it this way:**
- Agents read them via filesystem (no DB client needed in a shell session)
- Git-tracked → diffable per branch, reviewable in PRs, branches as worktrees
- Survives across machines without a shared DB
- `AGENTS.md` is in-repo for the same reason — agents pick instructions up from the working tree

**Why it doesn't hold up:**
- Your `feedback_port_review_antipatterns` memory literally calls out "JSON files as storage" — YAML is the same shape with prettier syntax
- Concurrent agents on parallel lanes editing the same `runs/` directory = merge conflicts on state. That's the exact failure mode a DB prevents.
- `INDEX.md` being hand-maintained is the giveaway — anytime a human (or an agent) has to remember to update an index after a state change, the source-of-truth is mis-architected.
- Querying "all blocked runs touching FS4" requires `grep`. With a DB it's a `WHERE`.
- Status transitions have no integrity constraints — nothing stops `verified → in_progress` or a `superseded_by` pointing at nothing.
- 39k lines of historical state in `git log` forever, vs. a single archived table.

**The only piece that legitimately belongs as files** is `AGENTS.md` + `agent_rules/*.md` — those are *instructions*, read at agent boot, naturally version-controlled with the code. The `runs/*.yaml` ledger should be a Postgres/SQLite table with a thin CLI for agents to read/write.

So your instinct is right: agent *instructions* on disk, agent *run state* in the DB. This PR conflates the two and ports the conflation onto `dev`.

> DEVELOPER

so what's the workaround to make these in the DB?

> AGENT

Shape it as a tiny DB-backed ledger with a CLI shim, so the agent ergonomics stay the same while the source of truth moves into Postgres/SQLite.

**Minimum viable wedge:**

1. **One `agent_runs` table** mirroring the YAML schema 1:1 — `run_id PK`, `status`, `branch`, `title`, `created_at`, `updated_at`, `supersedes_id FK`, `orchestrator_run_id FK`, `lane_id`, plus JSONB columns for the sub-objects that don't need querying (`source_artifacts`, `verification`, `execution_state`, `handoff`, `quality`). Real columns for the things you'd actually filter on (status, branch, run_id, FKs); JSONB for the prose.
2. **A thin CLI** — `uv run python -m tools.runs {create,update,get,list,close,export}`. Agents call it instead of `Edit`-ing YAML. `list --status in_progress,blocked` replaces the hand-maintained `INDEX.md`. `export <id>` dumps YAML on demand for review or attaching to a PR.
3. **`AGENTS.md` + `agent_rules/*.md` stay on disk** — those are instructions, correctly file-based.
4. **Migration**: one-shot script reads the existing 85 YAMLs into the table, then deletes `agent_rules/runs/*.yaml` from `dev` (the historical archive lives in git history if anyone ever needs it).

**Main trade-off:** agents in a bare shell with no DB connection can't read run state — they'd need to invoke the CLI which needs DB creds. On `dev_sprint`/`dev` workflows that's a non-issue since the app already has DB access; for a fresh clone on a new machine it's one more setup step. Worth it to kill the "85-YAML merge-conflict zone" problem and get real queries back.

**Wedge size:** ~1 migration + ~200 lines of CLI + ~50 lines of import script. Could land as one PR if you want to propose it as a counter to #271.

> DEVELOPER

I liteally don't underatdn any of this. Why is this not in a table to begin with what is happening I'm so confused

> AGENT

Fair, let me back up and just say it plainly.

**What happened, in plain English:**

Someone — almost certainly a Cursor or Codex agent session, not a human deliberately designing this — decided that when an agent works on a task, it should write a little YAML file describing what it's doing, why, what it's blocked on, what it tried, etc. Like a diary entry per task.

That diary lives in `agent_rules/runs/` as a file. When the task is done, the file gets moved to `agent_rules/runs/closed/`. There are now ~85 closed ones and 7 active ones. PR #271 is porting all 85 + the diary-writing rules onto `dev`.

**Why it's not in a table:**

Because no one designed it. It grew. The first agent probably wrote one YAML to keep notes on a long task. The next agent copied the pattern. Eventually it became a "schema" (note the `schema_version: 1` at the top — that's a tell that someone retroactively tried to formalize what was already sprawling). Now there's a whole `run_protocol.md` document telling future agents to keep doing it this way.

It's not in the database because:
1. Agents can write a file with one tool call. Writing to the database means knowing the schema, having credentials, picking an ORM, writing a CLI. **Files are the path of least resistance for an agent.**
2. No human reviewed the design at the start to say "wait, this is state, state goes in the DB."
3. By the time it was 20 YAML files it was easier to keep going than to migrate.
4. The agents writing these files probably didn't know your repo has a "DB-only storage" rule. Or they knew and rationalized "this is meta, not product."

**What it actually is:** a self-perpetuating agent bureaucracy. Agents read `AGENTS.md`, which tells them to write run records, so they write run records, which justifies the existence of `run_protocol.md`, which gets ported between branches in PRs like #271.

**Is any of it load-bearing?** Probably not for the product. The product (your FS10 validation code, the analytical pipeline, the database — the stuff you actually ship) doesn't read these YAMLs. They're just notes agents leave for the next agent.

**Should you care?** Only if:
- You don't want 39k lines of agent diary in `dev`'s git history forever, or
- You think future agents shouldn't be following the YAML-diary pattern when there's a DB right there.

If neither bothers you, close the tab and let Patrick decide. If both bother you, the answer is "reject this PR and tell whoever owns the agent workflow to put run state in the DB."

> DEVELOPER

ahhhhhs so this is agent runs not really runs from the pipeline I was so confused!

> AGENT

Yes, exactly that. Two totally different things sharing a confusing word.

- **Pipeline runs** = your actual product. An FS10 validation execution, an ingest job, a tuning run. Lives in the database (`runs` table, foreign keys, real state). This is what the system *does*.
- **Agent runs** = a Cursor/Codex agent's session notes while *writing the code that builds the pipeline*. Lives as YAML files in `agent_rules/runs/`. This is meta-work about building the system.

PR #271 is 100% the second kind. None of it touches the pipeline, the database, the validation logic, or anything your product actually executes. It's just agent-diary porting.

The naming collision is genuinely terrible and is probably why the YAML thing got as big as it did without anyone noticing — "runs" sounds important and product-shaped, so reviewers probably skimmed past it assuming it was related to actual pipeline runs.

## Summary

- Grade multiple conditional edges in parallel during step 3b (`batch_grade_conditional_edge_quality` uses a thread pool with per-edge isolation; each worker still runs the full 5-step Fermi chain sequentially).
- Add `packages/domain/edges/fs5_edge_grading.py` to resolve worker count from explicit arg, `ELIJAH_FS5_EDGE_MAX_WORKERS`, `[fs5_edge_grading]` in `config/run_profile.toml`, or model-set defaults (`local_slm` → 2, `openrouter_*` → 8).
- Split FS5 Fermi LLM tasks in `config/llm_tasks.toml`: steps 1–3 → `small`, steps 6–7 → `large` (meaningful on OpenRouter; no change on `local_slm` where all tiers map to Qwen 3B).
- Record `fs5_edge_max_workers` in `edge_grading.json`.

Multi-edge-in-one-prompt batching is **deferred** (out of scope).

cc @kungfusaini

## Test plan

- [x] `pytest tests/test_fs5_edge_grading_concurrency.py tests/test_fs5_edge_fermi_chain.py tests/test_conditional_edge_quality_store.py -q`
- [ ] Run step 3b on a question with multiple conditional edges; confirm `edge_grading.json` reports `fs5_edge_max_workers` and edges grade without cross-edge bleed
- [ ] On OpenRouter, spot-check that steps 6–7 route to the large tier model


Made with [Cursor](https://cursor.com)

`max_workers = 0` is being used as a **sentinel meaning "auto"**, not "zero workers / disabled."

The config comment makes it explicit:
```
# max_workers=0 or unset = auto from [llm].model_set
# (local_slm → 2, openrouter_* → 8). Override with ELIJAH_FS5_EDGE_MAX_WORKERS.
[fs5_edge_grading]
max_workers = 0
```

So the resolution chain when you ask "how many threads should grade edges in parallel?" goes:

1. Explicit argument passed in code → use it (if ≥ 1)
2. `ELIJAH_FS5_EDGE_MAX_WORKERS` env var → use it (if ≥ 1)
3. `[fs5_edge_grading].max_workers` from `run_profile.toml` → use it **if > 0**
4. Otherwise fall back to a default based on which model set you're running: `local_slm` → 2, `openrouter_*` → 8, else 4

Setting `0` in the shipped config means "I want the auto behavior, please pick based on whichever model backend is active." If they'd left it unset, same outcome. They wrote `0` explicitly so a reader sees the key exists and knows it's a knob they can change.

**Is this a good pattern?** Mildly questionable — `null` / commented-out / omitted would be more conventional than overloading `0`. But it's documented inline and the resolver code (`if explicit >= 1`) handles it consistently, so it's fine. Not a bug.

**The actual substance of the PR** is the real interesting bit: it parallelizes step 3b of FS5 edge grading using a thread pool, splits the 5-step Fermi chain so cheap steps (1-3) hit the `small` model tier and expensive steps (6-7) hit `large`, and isolates per-edge failures so one bad edge doesn't kill the batch. The `0`-as-auto is just a config ergonomics choice on top.

title:	Cross-FS: Stamp submitted_by + per-user job filtering (SPI-246)
state:	OPEN
author:	kungfusaini (Sumeet Saini)
labels:	
assignees:	
reviewers:	
projects:	
milestone:	
number:	270
url:	https://github.com/elijahintelligence/elijah/pull/270
additions:	868
deletions:	9
auto-merge:	disabled
--
## What this is

This PR makes every job in the system know who submitted it, and makes the GUI's job list show you only your own jobs (unless you're an admin, in which case you see everyone's). Today, every job in the database is anonymous — nothing on the row records who submitted it — and the GUI's "active jobs" view shows everyone's work to everyone.

With this PR, every job gets stamped with `submitted_by` at submit time, taken from the `User` that the CF ZT header bridging (PR #269) resolves to. The GUI list endpoints now filter by that field: non-admin users see only their own jobs, admins (the `admin: true` entries in `api_users.toml`) see everything automatically. There's no toggle and no extra UI element — admin status alone drives the unfiltered view, per the auth plan.

This is the second of three pieces of the demo-auth foundation. The run-cap ticket (next) will count against `submitted_by` at submit time to enforce the per-user `max_runs`.

## Technical detail

Stacked on PR #269 (SPI-243 PR-2 — CF ZT header bridging). Base branch for review: `sumeet/spi-243-pr2-cf-header-bridging`.

### Summary

- **Schema**: new nullable `submitted_by VARCHAR(255)` column on `jobs`, indexed. Alembic migration `20260519_01` (head bumped from `20260518_01`).
- **Contracts**: `JobAcceptedResponse.submitted_by` and `JobStatusResponse.submitted_by` added (both `str | None`, default `None`).
- **Storage**: `DatabaseJobQueue.submit_job(submitted_by=...)` persists it; new `list_jobs(submitted_by=..., limit=50)` and updated `list_active_jobs(submitted_by=..., limit=10)` filter on it.
- **Service**: `JobService.submit_research_job` / `submit_research_step_job` / `submit_research_batch_step_job` accept `submitted_by`, pass it down. New `JobService.list_jobs(submitted_by=..., limit=50)` returns `list[JobStatusResponse]`.
- **Routes**: `POST /api/jobs/research` and `POST /api/jobs/research-steps` depend on `get_current_user` and pass `submitted_by=user.user_id`. New `GET /api/jobs` returns the current user's recent jobs (admins see all). `GET /api/jobs/active` now applies the same filter.

### Approach

The filter rule: `submitted_by=None` to the queue layer means "no filter" (admin view); a concrete `user_id` string means "only this user's rows". Admins (via `User.is_admin`) get `submitted_by=None`; everyone else gets `user.user_id`. The route helpers `_submitter_id` and `_list_filter_for` encode this in one place so the same rule is applied at submit and at list.

Admin's own jobs **are** stamped with their `user_id` (e.g. `sumeet` in the example TOML). The admin flag affects the *list* view, not the *stamp* — so if an admin wants to see only their own jobs, that's a future filter parameter; today the rule is simply "admins see everything."

The legacy `require_api_key` gate on the router-include stays untouched. Routes now have two layers of identity:
1. `require_api_key` — the legacy gate on every router (`_api_auth = [Depends(require_api_key)]` in `services/api/app.py`). Returns `None` when `ELIJAH_API_KEY` is unset (dev-mode auth-disabled).
2. `get_current_user` — new per-route dependency on jobs routes. Resolves the `User` via CF header or X-API-Key (per PR-2's logic).

Both run; FastAPI dedups identical `Depends`. SPI-247 will rip out (1) entirely once every caller is identifiable through (2). For SPI-246, layering keeps the diff small and the existing tests stable.

Anonymous callers (auth disabled, or callers using the shared `ELIJAH_API_KEY` that resolves to the fallback `"shared"` user with no admin flag) leave `submitted_by=None` on submit and see the unfiltered list (since `current_user` is `None`). This is intentional for the demo deployment shape and matches what `require_api_key` would do alone.

Source plan: PR #266.

### Tests

8 new tests in `tests/test_api_submitted_by.py`, 2 classes:

`TestSubmittedByPersisted` (4 tests):
- Submit stamps the resolved `user_id` from the CF header.
- `JobStatusResponse` surfaces `submitted_by`.
- Admin submissions are also stamped (admin flag affects list view, not stamp).
- Unknown CF email auto-provisions and stamps with `user_id = email`.

`TestListJobsFiltering` (4 tests):
- Non-admin's `GET /api/jobs` returns only their own jobs.
- Admin's `GET /api/jobs` returns all jobs (multiple submitters).
- `GET /api/jobs/active` applies the same filter.
- Auto-provisioned users see only their own jobs.

Also updated `tests/test_fs5_odds_multiplier_head.py::test_alembic_has_single_head_with_latest_storage_migration` to expect the new head (`20260519_01`).

An autouse `_reset_rate_limiter` fixture resets the slowapi limiter between tests (it's a module-level singleton; without this, multiple submits across tests share the per-IP counter and hit 429).

### Verification

- [x] `uv run ruff check .` — clean
- [x] `uv run ruff format --check .` — 554 files already formatted
- [x] `uv run python -m pytest --ignore=tests/frontend -x -q` — 1383 passed, 8 skipped
  - Same transient `test_parallel_questions_faster_than_sequential` wall-clock-timing flake observed on every PR in this stack (asserts parallel execution < 0.6s; got 0.9s under load). Re-ran the file in isolation and all 11 tests pass cleanly. This refactor cannot affect parallel question-runner timing.
- [x] `tests/test_api_submitted_by.py` runs green on its own (8 passed)
- [x] `tests/test_api_auth.py` runs green on its own (44 passed)
- [ ] Postgres-targeted migration tests — not run locally; the migration is straightforward additive column + index and uses the same `_column_names` / `_index_names` idempotency helpers as recent migrations.
- [x] Frontend smoke test — not applicable; this PR is backend-only.

### Risks

Low-to-medium.

- **Migration**: new column is nullable; existing rows get `NULL`. Filter rule treats `NULL` rows as belonging to "no one" — for non-admin views they're hidden, for admin views they show up. If anyone backfills `submitted_by` from `request_data` later, that's a separate one-off.
- **List endpoint contract**: `GET /api/jobs` is new — no existing caller relies on it. Frontend will need a small update to render it, but no breaking change to existing endpoints.
- **`GET /api/jobs/active`**: this endpoint *does* exist today. Today it returns all jobs; now it returns the current user's jobs (or all, for admin). If any deployed frontend currently shows the unfiltered list, it will need to either become admin-routed or accept the per-user view. Acceptable behavior change for the demo per the auth plan; flagging.
- **Routes still gate on the legacy shared key**: by design (deferred to SPI-247). CF-authenticated callers and shared-key callers both work.

### Branch-merge

Stacked on PR #269. Should not merge until PR-2 lands. After PR-2 merges into `dev`, rebase this branch onto fresh `dev` so its diff is just SPI-246's changes.

### Out of scope (deliberately)

- Run-cap enforcement — separate ticket (new run-cap ticket in the demo-auth epic).
- LiteLLM-side per-user identity — SPI-244 (full-auth epic).
- Removing the legacy `require_api_key` gate — SPI-247 (full-auth epic).
- Backfilling `submitted_by` on existing jobs in deployed DBs.
- Frontend changes to render the new `submitted_by` field or the new `GET /api/jobs` endpoint — separate follow-up (the API contract is in place; the GUI work can land independently).

Source plan: #266
Stacked on: #269

It's a **merge-conflict resolution check-in** from agent hqmf8104.

When you do a merge (probably rebasing this PR onto fresh `dev` after PR #269 landed, or merging in commits from #272/#273 which are now on this branch), git hit a conflict in two versions of some "user details" — most likely in `config/api_users.toml` or the test fixtures, where:

- **"sumeet use details"** = this branch's version, which uses your real sumeet user (matches your `submitted_by=user.user_id` tests — non-admin path, auto-provisioned users, etc.)
- **"admin user details"** = the other side's version, which used a generic admin/admin-flagged user

The agent picked "keep what's on this branch" (sumeet user) and is asking @kungfusaini to confirm that was the right call before they finalize and merge the PR.

**Why they're asking:** SPI-246's whole point is the *non-admin path* — proving that non-admins see only their own jobs. If the conflict had been resolved the other way (admin user details win), the tests would technically still pass, but they'd exercise the admin "see everything" branch and lose coverage of the per-user filter rule that this PR exists to enforce. So picking the sumeet/non-admin version is almost certainly correct.

> AGENT

GitHub says **you** authored it — PR #273 and commit `830d3e4` are both signed as `kungfusaini`. It was merged by hqmf8104 (the agent) about an hour later.

**But the realistic picture:** PR #273 doesn't have the `Made with Cursor` tag that PRs #271 and #274 do, so the attribution is genuinely yours-on-paper. That means one of:

1. **You wrote it directly** (less likely given you're surprised right now)
2. **An agent session signed in as you** (Claude Code, Cursor, or Codex running with your GitHub credentials) wrote it under your account without tagging itself
3. **An agent prepared the diff and you committed/pushed it** without spotting the email

Option 2 is most consistent with everything else going on — the same broader workflow has agents (hqmf8104, plus your own delegated sessions) authoring and merging PRs across this stack. The agent that wrote `test_api_users_me.py` for the "GET /api/users/me" endpoint probably grabbed your real email from somewhere (your git config, the existing `api_users.toml`, or your earlier conversations) thinking it was a sensible "realistic-looking fixture" — and didn't know about your `57de59a` precedent that says "no real emails in tests."

**Net:** the commit is attributed to you, but the *behavior* (re-introducing a real email after you'd already cleaned it once) is consistent with an agent that wasn't aware of the precedent. Worth tightening your agent setup so test fixtures never use real emails — that would catch this category permanently.

| `services/worker/research_driver_step_pipeline.py` | content |
| `tests/test_research_driver_stage_artifacts.py` | content |

**Why these conflicts exist:** The PR is **stale and largely superseded**. Three of its four commits — `cdca560 "Persist research driver stage artifacts"`, `d1cd55d`, `e9ae1b9` — implement a feature that has *already been merged into `dev`* via two other PRs by other agents:

- **PR #243** `c537f0f` — "Storage/ResearchDriver: Add stage artifact authority"
- **PR #244** `ec20da2` — "Storage/ResearchDriver: Persist stage artifacts"

The add/add conflict on `research_driver_stage_artifact_persistence.py` is the smoking gun — two parallel agent implementations of the same module name.

**The one piece of genuinely new work** on PR #245 is the head commit `a83c263 "Read research driver artifacts from DB first"` — also the PR title. That's the part that adds value on top of what #243/#244 already landed: making the read path prefer the DB.

**Recommendation:** don't try to resolve these conflicts manually — there's nothing meaningful to reconcile because three of the four commits are duplicates of merged work. Either:

1. **Close PR #245 and re-do `a83c263` as a fresh PR** off current `dev`. Cleanest. ~5 files of real change.
2. Or rebase `codex/read-research-driver-stage-artifacts` onto `origin/dev` and `git reset --soft` the three duplicate commits away, leaving only `a83c263`'s changes to re-stage against the now-merged base. Same result, messier history.

**Wave 2 — Cluster B (FS4 baselines), tight ordering matters:**
6. **#262** FS10/Priors: crowd + GARCH target priors *(base of the stack)*
7. **#264** FS10/Priors: remove silent prior fallbacks *(already stacked on #262 — its base branch IS the #262 branch, so merge in this order)*
8. **#288** FS4: remove SLM stub from parallel race
9. **#280** Cross-FS: parallel FS5 grading + BN clamp + preflight — merge last in cluster, will need a small rebase after the above

**Wave 3 — Cluster C (domain routing):**
10. **#253** FS10/Routing: market-series domain routing
11. **#286** FS2/Routing: infer domain pack from question text

**Wave 4 — Auth (rebase first):**
12. **#270** Cross-FS: stamp submitted_by + per-user filtering — its parent #269 is already merged, so the CONFLICTING flag will clear after a rebase onto fresh `dev`. Includes your `fd5333c` email scrub.

**Wave 5 — needs rebase against merged work (or close):**
13. **#245** Storage/ResearchDriver: read artifacts from DB first → **close and re-open as a fresh PR** containing only commit `a83c263`. Three of its four commits duplicate already-merged PRs #243/#244.
14. **#248** FS4/FS5: indicator quality gate → rebase against merged stage-artifact work, then re-run
15. **#285** FS2/ResearchDriver: continue pipeline on retrieval_no_evidence → rebase
16. **#290** FS2: simplify FS2 web research retrieval → currently draft + conflicting; should be the last in Cluster A since it touches everything

**Defer / triage separately (not part of normal merge order):**
- **#271** agent workflow + 39k-line diary import — your call whether to merge at all (we discussed this; not load-bearing for the product)
- **#283** docs/plans consolidation — draft
- **#259** SLM end-to-end report — draft docs

## Why this order

- Wave 1 PRs touch entirely disjoint files; merging them never bumps the others.
- Cluster B is intentionally serialized 262 → 264 → 288 → 280 because each one builds on the baselines surface the previous one shipped. Skipping order means rebases.
- Cluster A is **the** problem zone — five PRs touching the same 3 pipeline files. The fastest path through it is to **first** close #245 (it's stale), **then** rebase 248/285/290 individually after Cluster B settles, because Cluster B also touches some of the same files via `dispatch.py`/`apply.py`.
- #270 deliberately sits in its own wave because the only thing keeping it CONFLICTING is the now-merged #269 — one rebase clears it.

**One real risk to flag:** merging #280 before #264 will break #264, and merging anything in Cluster A before Cluster B will likely re-conflict some of the baselines work. Stick to wave order.

    FS10/Priors: Harden crowd probability parsing

diff --git a/packages/domain/baselines/crowd_prior.py b/packages/domain/baselines/crowd_prior.py
index adb5280..616c2d7 100644
--- a/packages/domain/baselines/crowd_prior.py
+++ b/packages/domain/baselines/crowd_prior.py
@@ -58,10 +58,11 @@ def estimate_crowd_target_prior(
         )
 
     probability = min(max(probs[node_index], EPS), 1.0 - EPS)
+    confidence = _confidence_for_crowd_source(source)
     return BaselineEstimate(
         probability=probability,
         method="crowd_target_prior",
-        confidence="high",
+        confidence=confidence,
         as_of=as_of,
         sample_size=len(options),
         effective_sample_size=float(len(options)),
@@ -70,13 +71,20 @@ def estimate_crowd_target_prior(
             "source": source,
             "option_label": options[node_index],
             "option_index": node_index,
-            "crowd_probabilities": dict(zip(options, probs, strict=True)),
+            "raw_crowd_probabilities": dict(raw_probs),
+            "normalised_crowd_probabilities": dict(zip(options, probs, strict=True)),
         },
         notes=[f"Used {source} crowd-implied target probability as the primary prior."],
     )
 
 
 def _extract_probability_map(question_features: Mapping[str, Any], options: list[str]) -> tuple[dict[str, float], str]:
+    """Return the first non-empty crowd surface in fixed priority order.
+
+    ``crowd_probabilities`` wins over ``outcome_prices`` over GJOpen
+    ``consensus_targets``. When a question is live on multiple venues, only the
+    highest-priority source is used (no blending).
+    """
     for key, source in (
         ("crowd_probabilities", "crowd_probabilities"),
         ("outcome_prices", "polymarket_outcome_prices"),
@@ -114,19 +122,40 @@ def _parse_mapping(raw: Mapping[str, Any], options: list[str]) -> dict[str, floa
     return parsed
 
 
+def _confidence_for_crowd_source(source: str) -> str:
+    """Map crowd surface source to a defensibility confidence tier."""
+    if source in {"crowd_probabilities", "polymarket_outcome_prices"}:
+        return "high"
+    if source == "gjopen_consensus_targets":
+        return "medium"
+    return "medium"

"""FS4 dispatch — owns per-node-type orchestration and the mechanical selector.

Per FS4 §3 / §13.2, this module replaces the implicit cascade in `_tier_helpers.py`
with explicit per-node-type routing:

- **Targets** dispatch on `question.classification` (Num.Ord / Time.Ord / Cat / Bin)
  and run a sequential cascade. See FS4 §3.1.
- **Drivers / indicators** run a parallel race (SLM stub + configured estimators)
  and FS4's mechanical selector picks the highest-scored result. See FS4 §3.2 / §3.3.
- **Indicators** additionally check the IW gate before entering the race. If
  the indicator is IW-triggered, the race is skipped and a deterministic
  `BaselineEstimate(probability=1.0)` (or `0.0`) is returned. See FS4 §3.3.

W1-B-2 SCOPE: implements the dispatch bodies + mechanical selector + parallel
race infrastructure. Wiring into `apply.py` is deliberately deferred to a
follow-up PR (W1-B-3) so this PR stays review-tractable and behaviour-
preserving until Phase C estimators are ready.
"""

from __future__ import annotations
        return EstimationFailure(
            method="dispatch_selector",
            reason="invalid_output",
            details={
                "note": "No defensible non-fallback prior candidate produced an estimate.",
                "candidate_count": len(candidates.results),
                "success_count": len(successes),
                "candidates": [candidate.to_detail_payload() for candidate in candidates.results],
            },
        )

    # Candidates that have no defensibility score yet shouldn't reach the
    # selector, but if they do, treat them as score=0 (effectively last
    # in the ranking).
    def _score(c: BaselineEstimate) -> int:
        return c.defensibility.total if c.defensibility is not None else 0

    max_score = max(_score(c) for c in survivors)
    tied = [c for c in survivors if _score(c) == max_score]
    if len(tied) == 1:
        return tied[0]
    return _merge_tied_estimates(tied)


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Public dispatch entry points (FS4 §17.1).
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------


# Lazy-imported estimator callables — import at call time to avoid bringing
# heavy dependencies into module-level scope.


def _build_driver_race_estimators(
    *,
    node: Any,
    question_features: Mapping[str, Any],
    as_of: str,
    storage_config: StorageRuntimeConfig | None = None,
) -> list[tuple[str, Callable[[], EstimateResult]]]:
    """Build the parallel-race estimator list for a driver node.

    Composition:
    - SLM stub (`EstimationFailure(reason="not_implemented")`)
    - Main pipeline candidate: proxy tier, then configured LLM/Fermi tier.
      If neither tier can estimate, it returns `EstimationFailure`.
    """
    from .features import build_driver_features
    from .slm import estimate_slm_baseline

    def _slm_call() -> EstimateResult:
        return estimate_slm_baseline(
            node=node,
            role="driver",
            question_features=question_features,
            as_of=as_of,
        )

    def _main_pipeline_call() -> EstimateResult:
        from .driver import estimate_driver_baseline as _legacy_driver_pipeline
```

 | `inputs` | Dict of the features that drove the estimate (domain, region, horizon, ontology axes, proxy spec, …) |
 | `notes` | List of human-readable explanations, one per adjustment |
 
+## Driver and indicator parallel race (STEP_6A dispatch)
+
+STEP_6A calls [packages/domain/baselines/dispatch.py](../../packages/domain/baselines/dispatch.py), not the legacy tier modules directly. Per driver or indicator node, **two estimators** run in parallel, then FS4's mechanical selector picks the highest defensibility score:
+
+| Slot | Method label | Behaviour |
+|---|---|---|
+| 1 | `driver_main_pipeline` / `indicator_main_pipeline` | Legacy tier cascade in [driver.py](../../packages/domain/baselines/driver.py) / [indicator.py](../../packages/domain/baselines/indicator.py) (proxy → Fermi → ontology / 0.5) |
+| 2 | `driver_half` / `indicator_half` | Deterministic 0.5 safety fallback |
+
+A deferred small-tier (SLM) estimator was removed from the race while unimplemented; reintroduce it as a third parallel candidate when SPI-118 / M3 lands. This path is separate from STEP_6B target priors.
+
+Indicators check the **IW gate** before the race; IW-triggered nodes return deterministic `1.0` / `0.0` without racing.
+
+The sections below describe the **legacy tier cascade** inside the `*_main_pipeline` candidate.
+
 ## Driver estimation (3 tiers)
 
 Implementation: [packages/domain/baselines/driver.py](../../packages/domain/baselines/driver.py). Tiers evaluated in order; first one that succeeds wins.

90	| `version` | `"baseline_v1"` |
91	| `method` | Method ID (see per-node-type sections below) |
92	| `as_of` | ISO8601 UTC timestamp at estimation time |
93	| `estimate` | The `baseline_p` value written to the node (float, 6-dp rounded) |
94	| `confidence` | `"high"`, `"medium"`, or `"low"` |
95	| `sample_size` | Integer count of archive matches / proxy observations |
96	| `effective_sample_size` | Float weight after similarity weighting |
97	| `fallback_used` | `True` if a tier fell through to its safety-net |
98	| `inputs` | Dict of the features that drove the estimate (domain, region, horizon, ontology axes, proxy spec, …) |
99	| `notes` | List of human-readable explanations, one per adjustment |
100	
101	## Driver and indicator parallel race (STEP_6A dispatch)
102	
103	STEP_6A no longer calls the legacy tier modules directly. [packages/domain/baselines/dispatch.py](../../packages/domain/baselines/dispatch.py) runs a **parallel race** per driver or indicator node, then FS4's mechanical selector picks the highest defensibility score among survivors.
104	
105	For each node, up to **three estimators** run concurrently in a `ThreadPoolExecutor` (`max_workers=3`):
106	
107	| Slot | Method label | Behaviour |
108	|---|---|---|
109	| 1 | `driver_slm_stub` / `indicator_slm_stub` | SLM stub — returns `EstimationFailure(reason="not_implemented")` today |
110	| 2 | `driver_main_pipeline` / `indicator_main_pipeline` | Wraps the legacy tier cascade in [driver.py](../../packages/domain/baselines/driver.py) / [indicator.py](../../packages/domain/baselines/indicator.py) (proxy → Fermi → ontology / 0.5) |
111	| 3 | `driver_half` / `indicator_half` | Deterministic 0.5 safety fallback |
112	
113	`concurrent.futures.as_completed(..., timeout=…)` bounds the **wall-clock budget for the whole race** (default **60s**). When the budget expires, any estimator still running is recorded as `EstimationFailure(reason="model_error", details={"timeout": True})` and the selector proceeds with whichever candidates finished — typically the instant 0.5 fallback plus any fast failures. This is **not** the same path as STEP_6B's 2-way target prior pool.
114	
```

```
110	| 2 | `driver_main_pipeline` / `indicator_main_pipeline` | Wraps the legacy tier cascade in [driver.py](../../packages/domain/baselines/driver.py) / [indicator.py](../../packages/domain/baselines/indicator.py) (proxy → Fermi → ontology / 0.5) |
111	| 3 | `driver_half` / `indicator_half` | Deterministic 0.5 safety fallback |
112	
113	`concurrent.futures.as_completed(..., timeout=…)` bounds the **wall-clock budget for the whole race** (default **60s**). When the budget expires, any estimator still running is recorded as `EstimationFailure(reason="model_error", details={"timeout": True})` and the selector proceeds with whichever candidates finished — typically the instant 0.5 fallback plus any fast failures. This is **not** the same path as STEP_6B's 2-way target prior pool.
114	
115	**Root cause of `TimeoutError: N (of 3) futures unfinished`:** under LiteLLM load, the Fermi / main-pipeline slot (large or reasoning tier, e.g. DeepSeek R1) often exceeds the 60s race budget. Before this mitigation, `as_completed` raised `TimeoutError` and aborted the entire STEP_6A stage. Integration smoke (`fs10_val_q_126`) and multi-clone DeepSeek runs reproduced this at `driver_indicator_priors`.
116	
117	**Mitigations:**
118	
119	- Raise the race budget via `FS4_PARALLEL_RACE_TIMEOUT_SEC` (integration clones used `300` alongside `LLM_TIMEOUT_SECONDS=300` for slow reasoning models).
120	- Unfinished estimators are converted to timeout failures so the 0.5 fallback can still win instead of failing the step.
121	
122	Indicators additionally check the **IW gate** before entering the race; IW-triggered nodes skip the race and return a deterministic `1.0` / `0.0`.
123	
124	The sections below describe the **legacy tier cascade** inside the `*_main_pipeline` race candidate, not the dispatch wrapper.
125	
126	## Driver estimation (3 tiers)
127	
128	Implementation: [packages/domain/baselines/driver.py](../../packages/domain/baselines/driver.py). Tiers evaluated in order; first one that succeeds wins.
129	
130	### Tier 1 — `driver_proxy_frequency`
131	
132	If `node.data.detail.baseline_proxy` resolves to a spec via [proxy_catalog.py](../../packages/domain/baselines/proxy_catalog.py), the tier returns the historical frequency of the proxy series satisfying its comparator/threshold over the forecast horizon's window.
133	
134	- Comparators: `>`, `>=`, `<`, `<=`, `==`, `!=`
```

> AGENT

**Stopped on PR #280** — needs your input before I can responsibly auto-rebase.

**Status so far:**
- ✅ **#264** merged
- 🟡 **#288** rebased + pushed + commented; CI running (lint ✅, test/docker in progress). Non-trivial reconcile — the FS4 race now has a single candidate because #264 had also removed the `*_half` slot. Commented on the PR with details. *Recommendation: human eyeball before merge.*
- ⛔ **#280** rebase aborted

**Why I stopped on #280:**

1. **Substantive semantic conflict.** Commit `21eae94 "Emit prior_origin_warning for LLM-only baselines"` adds **107 lines** to `apply.py` and produces **6 conflict markers** against #264's rewrite of the same file. The interaction between "warn when only LLM baselines survive" and #264's "remove silent prior fallbacks" needs a design call — they're touching the same decision surface from opposite directions.

2. **PR bundles 9 commits across 3 unrelated concerns** (FS5 parallel grading, BN clamp, preflight hints), which violates your `CLAUDE.md` one-logical-change rule. The cleanest path is to split it into 3 PRs before rebasing — the FS5 parts probably rebase cleanly; only the FS10 baseline-warning piece carries the conflict.

**Suggested options for #280:**
- (a) Ask hqmf8104 to split into 3 PRs and re-rebase each separately
- (b) I rebase the easy 8 commits and skip `21eae94`, leaving the LLM-warning piece for a follow-up PR
- (c) You make the design call on how "prior_origin_warning" should compose with #264's no-silent-fallbacks model, then I apply

> DEVELOPER

so what's the issue with 280 I dont get it

> AGENT

Plain version:

**Two PRs touched the same file with overlapping ideas.**

- **PR #264 (just merged):** "If you can't compute a real prior from evidence, raise an error. Stop silently filling in fake values."
- **PR #280's commit `21eae94`:** "Even when a prior succeeds, if every surviving baseline came from the LLM (no historical data, no market data), emit a warning so eval runs can flag it."

Both are sensible. They're not contradictory. **But** both PRs rewrote the same ~100-line decision block in `packages/domain/baselines/apply.py` to insert their own logic. Each assumed it was the only edit happening there.

When I try to combine them, git can't tell which version's structure should win or where each piece slots in. It's not "this line vs that line" — it's "two completely different rewrites of the same chunk."

To merge them correctly, someone needs to decide:
- After #264's "fail loudly" path runs, where does the "warn when LLM-only" check go?
- Does the warning fire before or after the strict-fail check?
- If the strict fail already aborted, is the warning even reachable?

That's a design call. I can mechanically pick one side and squish them together, but I'd be guessing at intent, and getting it wrong would mean wrong warnings (or no warnings) firing in production runs.

**Plus there's a separate hygiene issue:** PR #280 actually bundles **9 commits about 3 unrelated things**:
- FS5 edge grading parallelization (5 commits) — unrelated to baselines
- BN clamp (1 commit) — unrelated
- Preflight CLI hints (2 commits) — unrelated
- **The LLM-only warning (1 commit) — this is the only one that conflicts**

So 8 of the 9 commits would rebase cleanly. Only the warning commit is stuck.

**My recommendation:** I rebase + push #280 with that one warning commit dropped, leaving a note for hqmf8104 to re-open the LLM-only warning as its own small PR against the post-#264 codebase where they can think about the interaction properly. That gets ~89% of #280's value into dev now and isolates the design question.

Want me to do that?

Status of all four, with what's actually blocking each:

## #245 — Storage/ResearchDriver: Read artifacts from DB first

**4 commits, but 3 are duplicates** of work already merged via #243/#244:
- `cdca560`, `d1cd55d`, `e9ae1b9` → duplicate stage-artifact persistence (merged as `c537f0f` + `ec20da2`)
- `a83c263` → the only novel commit ("DB-first read")

**4 conflicts**, including an `add/add` on `research_driver_stage_artifact_persistence.py` — that's the smoking gun: two parallel agent implementations of the same module.

**Fix:** close PR, re-open a fresh PR with only `a83c263` cherry-picked on top of current `dev`. Cleaner than a 4-commit untangle.

---

## #248 — FS4/FS5: Add indicator quality gate

**4 commits, 1 conflict** — and the conflict is **trivial**:
- Only file: `tests/test_maintainability_baseline.py`
- That test enforces line-count budgets per file. When two PRs both grow the same file, each bumps the budget; git can't auto-pick which budget value wins. Resolution = "take the higher of the two numbers."

⚠️ One commit (`c3a04c1 "Require clarifying questions when creating plans"`) looks **unrelated** to the indicator quality gate. May be a stowaway from another work session.

**Fix:** straightforward rebase — pick the higher budget number, possibly drop the unrelated commit.

---

## #280 — Cross-FS: Parallel FS5 grading, BN clamp, preflight hints

**9 commits across 3 unrelated concerns** (FS5 parallel grading × 5, BN clamp × 1, preflight CLI × 2, FS10 prior-origin warning × 1).

**1 conflict** — `packages/domain/baselines/apply.py` — but it's **substantive**, not textual. Commit `21eae94 "Emit prior_origin_warning for LLM-only baselines"` adds 107 lines to the same decision block that #264 rewrote ("remove silent prior fallbacks"). Both made independent rewrites; merging needs a design call about how warnings compose with strict-failure.

**Fix options:**
- (a) Rebase with `21eae94` skipped, reopen warning as its own PR — gets 8/9 commits landing now
- (b) Ask hqmf8104 to split into 3 PRs by concern
- (c) Wait for a design call on how the warning should compose with #264

---

## #285 — FS2/ResearchDriver: Continue pipeline on retrieval_no_evidence

**3 commits, 1 conflict** — and again it's **trivial**:
- Only file: `tests/test_maintainability_baseline.py` — same budget-bump conflict as #248

**Fix:** straightforward rebase, same "pick the higher number" resolution.

---

## TL;DR

| PR | Difficulty | Recommendation |
|---|---|---|
| #245 | 🔴 Surgery | Close + reopen as fresh PR with `a83c263` only |
| #248 | 🟢 Trivial | Standard rebase (budget bump) |
| #280 | 🟡 Design call | Either split, or rebase skipping `21eae94` |
| #285 | 🟢 Trivial | Standard rebase (budget bump) |
